UAT:
- Cloud SQL:

* Instance: me-2-uat-sql
* Backup Tier: Standard Tier
* Automated backups: Disabled

Steps:
1. Gcloud console
2. Project: me-2-uat
3. Cloud SQL
4. Instance: me-2-uat-sql
5. All instances > me-2-uat-sql > Backups
6. Backup Tier: Standard Tier
7. Create Backup
8. Once created: You will get an option to restore the instance to that timetamp

Production:
----
- Cloud SQL
* Instance: me-2-prod-sql
* Backup Tier: Standard Tier
* Automated backups: Enabled
* Backup window: Everyday 8:30 PM - 12:30 AM
* Automated backups - 7 days
* Point in time recovery - Enabled: 7 days(retention period)


Steps to be followed for Production Instance:
----

1. Project: me-2-prod
2. Cloud SQL Instance: me-2-prod-sql
3. All instances > me-2-prod-sql > Backups
4. Backup Tier: Standard Tier
5. Create Backup
6. Once created: You will get an option to restore the instance to that timetamp

Production Deployment Commands - MCP-P3 (North.Virginia)
-------

# Build
docker build -f DockerFile --platform linux/amd64 --build-arg REGION=us-east-1 --build-arg APP_ENV=production --build-arg NODE_ENV=production --build-arg CLOUD_PROVIDER=aws -t prod-vc-mcp .

# Tag
docker tag prod-vc-mcp:latest 520680734372.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/prod-vc-mcp:prod_v1.0

# ECR Login
aws ecr get-login-password --region us-east-1 | docker login --username AWS --password-stdin 520680734372.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com

# Push
docker push 520680734372.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/prod-vc-mcp:prod_v1.0

# Pull and run in EC2
docker pull 520680734372.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/prod-vc-mcp:prod_v1.0 && docker stop prod-vc-mcp || true && docker rm prod-vc-mcp || true && docker run -d --name prod-vc-mcp -e REGION=us-east-1 -p 4301:4300 520680734372.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/prod-vc-mcp:prod_v1.0 && docker logs -f prod-vc-mcp

prev = head
curr = head->next
next = head->next->next

head->next = null; ^BBMGjV5r

while(next)

    curr = next

Platform & Migration Infrastructure
-----

* Set up and supported migration platforms across GCP and AWS, including the two AWS regions.
* Worked on region-specific infrastructure, networking, Redis, MongoDB, Pub/Sub/SQS, load balancers, storage and deployment components.
* Supported the migration/onboarding of customers into the new platform architecture.
* Took ownership of troubleshooting infrastructure issues across cloud environments rather than limiting work to application-level changes. ^ZO7LUVQJ

3. Security, Coding Standards & Audit Readiness

* Worked on security-related code improvements and audit findings.
* Addressed vulnerabilities identified through tools such as AWS Inspector/ECR scanning and SCA/security tooling.
* Helped collect and establish evidence required during audits. ^ecJHCjDe

4. CI/CD & Engineering Process Improvements

One of the areas where I feel I contributed beyond regular development was improving our deployment process.

Then explain:

* Worked on implementing/improving CI/CD across environments.
* Set up and enhanced GCP Cloud Build and AWS deployment pipelines.
* Standardized deployment flows and environment-specific configurations.
* Reduced manual deployment effort and dependency on individuals.
* Initially there was some resistance because setting up the pipelines required additional effort, but I pushed the process forward because the long-term benefit was repeatability and reduced deployment risk.

Impact: Moved deployments toward a more repeatable and automated engineering process and reduced operational dependency on manual steps.

- GCP & central account needed. ^prODnEyk

So coming to production deployment, Each release is documented with a release ticket and a release page, which cover the scope, approvals, deployment plan, rollback plan and a readiness checklist.

As Rushitha mentioned, once the pull request to the production branch is approved, a dedicated engineer starts the builds and keeps them ready for the release. Once the release is approved, an authorised engineer deploys during a scheduled release window. The team then verifies the release and records the results in the post-deployment verification checklist.

Our key controls are mandatory pull request reviews, a minimum of two approvals for production changes, and release owner sign-off before deployment. Our environment branches are protected and can only be changed through approved pull requests, and only authorised personnel can deploy to production. Every release goes through UAT validation, a readiness checklist and post-deployment verification, each with a named verification owner.

We always keep a stable previous version of every service. If a rollback is needed, containers go back to the last stable image and serverless functions go back to the previous version. Every rollback is recorded with the reason followed by the version restoration and verification of result

Now let me show you how we do an actual production deployment.

Each feature -> Owner -> Responsible e2e

The web app version -> this is the tag for this service for todays release.
